<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We construct a contractible domain in complex dimension three with a complete Kähler metric whose real sectional curvatures lie between two finite negative constants. It admits no bounded holomorphic map to ℂ<sup>3</sup> with nowhere-vanishing Jacobian and is therefore not biholomorphic to a bounded domain. This gives a negative answer to the negatively pinched Kähler uniformization question.

- **claim-main-counterexample** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-main-theorem)): There exist a contractible domain $M\subset\mathbb C^3$, a smooth complete Kähler metric $g$ on $M$, and finite constants $0<A\le B$ such that $$-B\le K_g(\sigma)\le -A<0$$ for every real two-plane $\sigma\subset T_pM$ and every $p\in M$. No bounded holomorphic map $F:M\to\mathbb C^3$ has a nowhere-vanishing Jacobian determinant. In particular, $M$ is not biholomorphic to a bounded domain in $\mathbb C^3$.
  - Explanation: A contractible domain in complex dimension three carries a complete Kähler metric whose real sectional curvatures are squeezed between two finite negative constants. No bounded holomorphic map to complex three-space has a nowhere-vanishing Jacobian on it, so the domain is not biholomorphic to a bounded domain.
- **claim-controlled-potential** (theorem, [source](#stmt-base-existence)): There exist $\phi\in C^\infty(\mathbb B,\mathbb R)$ and a finite constant $C$ for which <a href="#base:control" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="base:control">[base:control]</a> and <a href="#base:jets" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="base:jets">[base:jets]</a> hold with $c=1$, and such that no $H\in\mathcal O(\mathbb B)$ and $C_H\in\mathbb R$ satisfy $$\label{base:no-minorant} \operatorname{Re}H(z) \le C_H+4\log\frac1{1-\|z\|}+\phi(z) \qquad(z\in\mathbb B).$$
  - Explanation: On the unit ball of complex dimension two there is a smooth real potential with uniformly controlled Hessian and jets in centered charts, which no holomorphic real part can lie below, even after adding the logarithmic boundary allowance.
- **claim-hartogs-metric-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-metric-transfer)): Let $\phi\in C^\infty(\mathbb B,\mathbb R)$ satisfy <a href="#base:control" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="base:control">[base:control]</a> and <a href="#base:jets" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="base:jets">[base:jets]</a> for fixed $0<c\le C<\infty$. There is a finite $\lambda_0=\lambda_0(c,C)>0$ such that, for every $\lambda\ge\lambda_0$, the domain $M_\phi$ in <a href="#metric:domain" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="metric:domain">[metric:domain]</a> is contractible and the tensor $g_\lambda=2\operatorname{Re}\widehat g_\lambda$ defined by <a href="#metric:potential" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="metric:potential">[metric:potential]</a> is a smooth geodesically complete Kähler metric. For this fixed $\lambda$ there are constants $0<A_\lambda\le B_\lambda<\infty$ such that $$-B_\lambda\le K_{g_\lambda}(\sigma)\le-A_\lambda<0$$ for every point of $M_\phi$ and every real two-plane $\sigma$ in its tangent space.
  - Explanation: Control of the base potential's Hessian and jets, together with a large enough base weight, yields a contractible Hartogs domain with a complete Kähler metric whose real sectional curvature is pinched between two negative constants.
- **claim-jacobian-obstruction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-map-obstruction)): Let $\phi\in C^\infty(\mathbb B,\mathbb R)$ have the nonexistence property in Proposition <a href="#base:existence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="base:existence">2</a>: no holomorphic $H$ and real constant $C_H$ satisfy <a href="#base:no-minorant" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="base:no-minorant">[base:no-minorant]</a>. Then no bounded holomorphic map $F:M_\phi\to\mathbb C^3$ has a nowhere-vanishing complex Jacobian determinant.
  - Explanation: If the potential admits no holomorphic real part below it up to a constant and the boundary allowance, then Cauchy estimates for a putative Jacobian along the zero section give a contradiction, so no bounded map has everywhere nonzero Jacobian.
- **claim-representing-mean-criterion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-peaks-mean-criterion)): Let $u$ be a continuous real function on $\mathbb B$. Suppose there are radii $0<r_N<1$ and representing probabilities $\mu_N$ on $S$ such that $$\int_S u(r_N\xi)\,d\mu_N(\xi)\longrightarrow-\infty.$$ Then no holomorphic function $H$ on $\mathbb B$ and constant $C_H$ satisfy $\operatorname{Re}H\le C_H+u$ on $\mathbb B$.
  - Explanation: If measures that reproduce holomorphic evaluation at the origin send the integral of a continuous function to minus infinity along suitable radii, then no holomorphic real part can lie below that function plus a constant.
- **claim-peak-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-peaks-bounds)): There are absolute constants $C,c>0$, independent of $D_0\ge1$, such that $$\label{peaks:count} \frac{k}{D_0^2}\le N_k\le\frac{4k}{D_0^2}$$ and, for every $\xi\in S$ and $u\ge0$, $$\label{peaks:gaussian} \sum_{\substack{p\in\mathcal P_k\\\sqrt{k}d(\xi,p)\ge u}} \|\langle\xi,p\rangle\|^k\le Ce^{-cu^2}.$$ Consequently, $$\label{peaks:global} \|P_k(z)\|\le C\|z\|^k\quad(z\in\mathbb C^2), \qquad \int_S\|P_k\|\,d\sigma\le\frac{2N_k}{k+2}.$$
  - Explanation: The number of separated line centers is comparable to the degree divided by the squared spacing, and the sums of powers of inner products have Gaussian tails, giving a global bound and an integral bound for the peak polynomial.
- **claim-patch-estimates** (theorem, [source](#stmt-peaks-patches)): Fix $R_0>0$. If $D_0>\max\{1,2\sqrt{2R_0}\}$ and $k\ge\max\{D_0^2,2R_0\}$, the patches $\mathcal U_{k,p}$ are pairwise disjoint. On each patch, $$\label{peaks:patch-measure} d\sigma=\frac2k e^{-2y/k}\,dy\, \frac{d\theta}{2\pi}\frac{d\gamma}{2\pi},$$ and $$\label{peaks:single-peak} \|P_k(\xi)-e^{-y}e^{ik\theta}\| \le C e^{-c(D_0-\sqrt{2R_0})^2}.$$ Outside their union, $$\label{peaks:off-patch} \|P_k(\xi)\|\le Ce^{-cR_0}.$$ The constants $C,c$ can be chosen independently of $R_0,D_0,k$.
  - Explanation: With adequate spacing the patches around peaks are disjoint, the sphere measure has an explicit form on each patch, each patch sees essentially a single peak, and the polynomial is small off the patches.
- **claim-radial-profiles** (theorem, [source](#stmt-peaks-profiles)): Fix $1<a<2$ and put $\alpha=\log a$. One can choose $0<s_1<1$, smooth real functions $f,b$ on $[0,\infty)$, and $R_0>0$ such that $$\label{peaks:profile-negativity} \alpha^2-2s_1(\cosh\alpha-1)<0,$$ $$\label{peaks:profile-identities} \\|f\\|_\infty<1,\quad f=-s_1\ \text{on }[0,\alpha],\quad \int_0^\infty e^{-y}f(y)\,dy=0,\quad b'-b=f,\quad b(0)=0.$$ Both functions vanish on a neighborhood of $[R_0,\infty)$. Moreover, $R_0$ can be chosen so that $\|aP_k\|\le1/2$ off the patches whenever the hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#peaks:patches" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="peaks:patches">7</a> hold.
  - Explanation: Smooth compactly supported radial profiles exist with a plateau, an exact weighted cancellation, a companion function solving a first-order relation, and a strict negativity inequality needed for the logarithmic tests.
- **claim-density-smoothness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-density-smooth)): Every $W_j$ defined by <a href="#density:recursion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="density:recursion">[density:recursion]</a> is a smooth real function on $S$.
  - Explanation: Every density produced by the recursion is smooth on the sphere, including along the central circles of the patches and across patch boundaries.
- **claim-uniform-representing-densities** (theorem, [source](#stmt-density-main)): There is a constant $K_*>0$, depending only on the fixed profiles $f,b$ and $R_0$, with the following property. For every fixed $D_0>\max\{1,2\sqrt{2R_0}\}$ there is an integer threshold $Q_0$ such that, for every integer $Q\ge Q_0$ and every choice of the separated sets above, the recursion <a href="#density:recursion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="density:recursion">[density:recursion]</a> has the following properties for all $j\ge1$:* 1. *$W_j$ is smooth and strictly positive, and $W_j\,d\sigma$ represents holomorphic evaluation at zero. In particular it is a probability measure.* 2. *Under global phase rotation, $W_j$ has frequencies only between $-\ell_j$ and $\ell_j$, and its mean on every phase orbit is exactly one.* 3. *At every point of $S$, $$\label{density:uniform-bounds} \begin{gathered} c_0W_{j-1}\le W_j\le2W_{j-1},\\ \|ZW_j\|\le K_*\sqrt{k_j}\,W_j, \qquad \|TW_j\|\le K_*k_jW_j \end{gathered}$$ for every unit horizontal vector $Z$.* *The threshold $Q_0$ may also be enlarged to impose any fixed lower threshold on the degrees $k_j$.
  - Explanation: For a large enough degree ratio, the recursion gives strictly positive smooth densities that represent holomorphic evaluation at zero, have controlled phase frequencies and unit orbit means, and satisfy uniform multiplicative and derivative bounds at every stage.
- **claim-moment-preservation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-density-moments)): For every stage of <a href="#density:recursion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="density:recursion">[density:recursion]</a>, the phase bandwidth of $W_j$ is at most $\ell_j$, its orbitwise mean is one, and $$\label{density:representing} \int_S p(\xi)W_j(\xi)\,d\sigma(\xi)=p(0) \quad\text{for every holomorphic polynomial }p.$$ Each individual correction has zero integral against every such polynomial, including the constant polynomial.
  - Explanation: Each correction has no zero frequency and annihilates every holomorphic polynomial by radial integration by parts, so the phase bandwidth, orbit mean and holomorphic moments are preserved at every stage.
- **claim-phase-orbit-growth** (bound, [source](#stmt-density-orbit-growth)): Suppose $Q\ge2$, $k_j=Q^j$, and the positive functions $W_0=1,W_1,\ldots,W_n$, with $n\ge1$, satisfy $$c_0W_{j-1}\le W_j\le2W_{j-1}, \qquad \|TW_j\|\le K_*k_jW_j \quad(1\le j\le n).$$ Put $$R=\frac2{c_0},\qquad \beta=\log_2R.$$ For every $p\in S$, the function $S_p(\theta)=W_n(e^{i\theta}p)$ satisfies $$\label{density:orbit-growth-bound} S_p(\theta+u) \le e^{K_*}R(1+k_n\|u\|)^\beta S_p(\theta), \qquad \|u\|\le\pi.$$ In particular, the constants in this bound are independent of $Q,n,p$.
  - Explanation: Under the stagewise comparison and phase-derivative bounds, the restriction of the density to a phase orbit grows at most polynomially in the frequency scale times the displacement, with constants independent of the stage.
- **claim-weighted-derivative-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-density-weighted-derivative)): Fix $A\ge1$ and $\beta\ge0$. Let $q\ge1$ be an integer, and let $s$ be a positive trigonometric polynomial of degree at most $2q$. Suppose that $$s(\theta+u)\le A(1+q\|u\|)^\beta s(\theta) \qquad(\theta\in\mathbb R,\ \|u\|\le\pi).$$ There is a constant $C(A,\beta)$, independent of $q,s$, such that $$\|s''(\theta)\|\le C(A,\beta)q^2s(\theta).$$
  - Explanation: A positive trigonometric polynomial with controlled growth along the circle has second derivative bounded by a constant times the squared degree scale times its own value, proved by convolution with a smoothed kernel.
- **claim-normalized-correction-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-density-normalized-patch)): Write $$F=\\|f\\|_\infty,\quad B=\\|b\\|_\infty,\quad F_1=\\|f'\\|_\infty,\quad B_1=\\|b'\\|_\infty.$$ On a degree-$k$ patch suppose $W_{j-1}>0$, $S_p>0$, and $$\frac{e^{2y/k}S_p}{W_{j-1}}\le2,\qquad r_1=\frac{\|S_p'\|}{kS_p}\le1,\qquad r_2=\frac{\|S_p''\|}{k^2S_p}\le1.$$ For the correction $A_{j,p}$ in <a href="#density:recursion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="density:recursion">[density:recursion]</a>, $$\|YA_{j,p}\|\le C_2W_{j-1}, \quad Y=Z/\sqrt{k}\ \hbox{or}\ T/k, \qquad C_2=2D_R\bigl[4(F+B)+F_1+B_1\bigr].$$ In particular, $C_2$ is independent of $K_*,Q,j,p$.
  - Explanation: On a patch, under normalized comparison and derivative ratios, the horizontal and phase derivatives of a single correction are bounded by a constant times the previous density, with a constant fixed by the profile data alone.
- **claim-exact-averaging** (theorem, [source](#stmt-tests-averaging)): Let $k=k_j=Q^j$, where $Q$ satisfies the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#density:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="density:main">10</a>. For $0\le r\le1$, $$\label{tests:averaging-identity} \int_S U_k(r\xi)W_j(\xi)\,d\sigma = \int_S U_k(r\xi)\,d\sigma +\sum_p\int_{\mathcal U_{k,p}} U_k(r\xi)e^{2y/k}f(y)\cos(k\theta)\,d\sigma.$$
  - Explanation: Phase orthogonality reduces the average of the test function against the current density to its plain sphere average plus a patchwise cosine-weighted term, because only the resonant mode survives.
- **claim-negative-average-one-scale** (bound, [source](#stmt-tests-negative)): There exist $\epsilon>0$, $\eta\in(0,1/2)$ and $D_*\ge1$, depending only on the fixed profile data, with the following property. For every $D_0\ge D_*$ there are $k_*>0$ and $d_2>0$ such that, for every integer $Q$ satisfying the density theorem and $Q\ge k_*$, $$\label{tests:one-scale-negative} \int_S U_{k_j}(r\xi)W_j(\xi)\,d\sigma\le-d_2 \qquad \bigl(j\ge1,\quad 1-\eta\le r^{k_j}\le1\bigr).$$ The constants $k_*,d_2$ may depend on $D_0$, but are independent of $j$ and of further increases in $Q$.
  - Explanation: A suitable regularization and spacing give a uniformly negative upper bound for the average of the degree-matched test function against its density, on a range of radii near the boundary.
- **claim-negative-average-later** (bound, [source](#stmt-tests-later)): Fix $\epsilon,\eta,D_0,k_*,d_2$ as in Proposition <a href="#tests:negative" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tests:negative">16</a>, and put $d_3=d_2/2$. There is a threshold $Q_*$ such that, for every integer $Q\ge Q_*$, the densities satisfy $$\label{tests:later-negative} \int_S U_{k_j}(r\xi)W_N(\xi)\,d\sigma\le-d_3 \qquad \bigl(N\ge j\ge1,\quad1-\eta\le r^{k_j}\le1\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: The negative bound survives against all later densities, since the later increments only carry high frequencies that are orthogonal to a low-frequency truncation of the test function.
- **claim-smooth-convergence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-base-convergence)): For every finite $L>0$, the series in <a href="#base:potential" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="base:potential">[base:potential]</a> converges locally in $C^\infty(\mathbb B)$. Its sum is smooth, satisfies $\phi(0)=0$, and has complex Hessian $h\ge I$.
  - Explanation: The series defining the base potential converges locally in the smooth topology, its sum vanishes at the origin, and its complex Hessian dominates that of the ball potential.
- **claim-no-pluriharmonic-minorant** (theorem, [source](#stmt-base-minorant)): If $$\label{base:amplitude} L>\frac{5\log Q}{d_3},$$ there are no $H\in\mathcal O(\mathbb B)$ and $C_H\in\mathbb R$ satisfying <a href="#base:no-minorant" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="base:no-minorant">[base:no-minorant]</a>.
  - Explanation: When the amplitude is large relative to the degree ratio and the one-scale negativity constant, the weighted averages tend to minus infinity and no holomorphic real part can lie below the potential plus the logarithmic allowance.
- **claim-uniform-chart-estimates** (bound, [source](#stmt-base-chart-estimates)): For every fixed finite $L>0$, the potential <a href="#base:potential" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="base:potential">[base:potential]</a> has uniformly bounded positive-order derivatives through order four at the origins of all centered ball charts. Consequently its complex Hessian satisfies <a href="#base:control" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="base:control">[base:control]</a> and <a href="#base:jets" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="base:jets">[base:jets]</a>, with $c=1$ and a finite constant $C$.
  - Explanation: Low degrees vary little in a centered chart and high degrees are exponentially small, so derivatives of the potential through the fourth order are uniformly bounded at all chart origins, giving the Hessian control and jet bounds required by the metric construction.
- **claim-hartogs-complete** (theorem, [source](#stmt-metric-complete)): Let $\phi$ be a smooth real plurisubharmonic function on $\mathbb B$, and let $\lambda>0$. The domain $M_\phi$ in <a href="#metric:domain" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="metric:domain">[metric:domain]</a> is contractible, and the complex Hessian of $\mathcal K_\lambda=\lambda\psi-\log(1-e^\phi\|w\|^2)$ defines a smooth geodesically complete Kähler metric $g=2\operatorname{Re}\widehat g$ on $M_\phi$. Moreover, $\mathcal K_\lambda$ is a smooth strictly plurisubharmonic exhaustion of $M_\phi$.
  - Explanation: For any smooth plurisubharmonic base function and positive base weight, the Hartogs domain is contractible and the Hessian of the potential gives a smooth geodesically complete Kähler metric with a strictly plurisubharmonic exhaustion.
- **claim-curvature-tensor** (theorem, [source](#stmt-curv-tensor)): In the frame just described, define the horizontal Hermitian form $$D=(1+2x)h-qhG^{-1}h.$$ The full curvature tensor is $$\label{curv:tensor-identity} R=-\lambda S_I-qS_h-S_V -(S_{D+V}-S_D-S_V)+T_4+T_3,$$ where $T_j$ has exactly $j$ horizontal indices. Its nonzero components, up to the Kähler symmetries and conjugation, are $$\begin{aligned} (T_4)_{i\bar j k\bar l} &=-x h_{i\bar j,k\bar l} +x^2G^{p\bar e}h_{i\bar e,k}h_{p\bar j,\bar l}, \label{curv:four-horizontal-error}\\ (T_3)_{i\bar j k\bar0} &=\sqrt q\bigl(-h_{i\bar j,k} +xG^{p\bar e}h_{i\bar e,k}h_{p\bar j}\bigr). \label{curv:three-horizontal-error} \end{aligned}$$ Measured by the Euclidean component norms in this frame, uniformly for $\lambda\ge1$, $$\label{curv:component-errors} \\|T_4\\|\le C_4x,\qquad \\|T_3\\|\le C_3\sqrt q.$$ If $\lambda$ is at least the uniform upper bound for the eigenvalues of $h$ at centered-chart origins, then $$\label{curv:D-bounds} (1+x)h\le D\le(1+2x)h.$$
  - Explanation: In a pointwise gauge the full curvature tensor splits into explicit symmetric-product terms plus error tensors with four and three horizontal indices, whose sizes are controlled by the chart bounds, and the auxiliary horizontal form is sandwiched between multiples of the base form.
- **claim-real-plane-curvature** (theorem, [source](#stmt-curv-real-plane)): For the metric convention $g=2\operatorname{Re}\widehat g$ and the curvature convention <a href="#curv:coordinate-formula" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="curv:coordinate-formula">[curv:coordinate-formula]</a>, $$\label{curv:sectional} K_g(\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb R}\{U_1,U_2\}) =\frac{\mathcal H_R}{2a_{\widehat g}}.$$ Define the matrix $$\label{curv:xi-definition} \Xi^{a\bar b}=u_1^a\overline{u_2^b} -u_2^a\overline{u_1^b}.$$ For every tensor $T$ with the Kähler symmetries and Hermitian conjugation symmetry, $$\label{curv:xi-contraction} \mathcal H_T=-\frac12 T_{a\bar b c\bar d}\Xi^{a\bar b}\Xi^{c\bar d}.$$ For every Hermitian form $P$, $$\label{curv:S-contraction} \mathcal H_{S_P}=a_P+3b_P^2.$$
  - Explanation: The real sectional curvature of an arbitrary real two-plane equals a contracted curvature expression divided by twice the real Gram determinant, with contraction formulas for general tensors and for the symmetric product tensors.
- **claim-gram-inequalities** (bound, [source](#stmt-curv-gram)): For a positive semidefinite Hermitian form $P$, $a_P\ge b_P^2$. For the vertical form $V$ one has $a_V=b_V^2$. If $P$ is horizontal and positive semidefinite, $m_P$ is nonnegative, linear and monotone in $P$, and $$\label{curv:mixed-gram} m_P\ge2\sqrt{a_Pa_V}.$$ When $a_Pa_V>0$, equality in <a href="#curv:mixed-gram" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="curv:mixed-gram">[curv:mixed-gram]</a> holds exactly when the two real Gram matrices of the pair for $P$ and $V$ are proportional.
  - Explanation: Real Gram determinants of positive semidefinite forms dominate the squared imaginary pairing, and the mixed cross term is nonnegative, linear, monotone and bounded below by twice the geometric mean of the individual determinants, with an equality criterion.
- **claim-contracted-errors** (bound, [source](#stmt-curv-errors)): For every real linearly independent tangent pair, $$\label{curv:contracted-errors} \|\mathcal H_{T_4}\|\le Cx a_I,\qquad \|\mathcal H_{T_3}\|\le C\sqrt q\,\sqrt{a_I m_I}.$$ More explicitly, the three-horizontal error satisfies $$\label{curv:mixed-error} \|\mathcal H_{T_3}\| \le C\sqrt q\,\\|\Xi_{HH}\\|\, \\|\Xi_{\mathrm{mix}}\\|.$$
  - Explanation: The contractions of the four-horizontal and three-horizontal error tensors are bounded in terms of the horizontal and mixed Gram quantities of the plane, even when a projected area vanishes.
- **claim-mixed-plane-algebra** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pinch-algebra)): Let $h$ be a positive definite horizontal Hermitian form, let $E$ be another horizontal Hermitian form with $E\ge h$, and let $u_1,u_2$ be real-linearly independent vectors in $\mathbb C^2\oplus\mathbb C$. Define $$P_h(E)=a_h+3b_h^2+4a_V+m_E+6b_Eb_V.$$ If $a_ha_V=0$, then $P_h(E)>0$. If $a_ha_V>0$, put $\tau=\|b_h\|/\sqrt{a_h}$. For every real $\beta$ such that $E\le(2-\beta)h$, $$\label{pinch:square} P_h(E)\ge \left(\sqrt{a_h+3b_h^2}-2\sqrt{a_V}\right)^2 +2\beta(1+\tau)\sqrt{a_ha_V}.$$ In particular, $h\le E\le2h$ implies $P_h(E)\ge0$. If equality holds in this last conclusion, then $$a_h=a_V>0,\qquad \|b_h\|=\sqrt{a_h},\qquad b_Eb_V<0,$$ and both mixed blocks $\Xi^{i\bar0}$ and $\Xi^{0\bar i}$ vanish.
  - Explanation: The leading algebraic expression of the curvature numerator is nonnegative on all real planes in the stated range, positive when a projected area vanishes, and its zeros force specific equalities and the vanishing of both mixed blocks.
- **claim-uniform-negativity-large-lambda** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pinch-positivity)): Fix $0<c\le C<\infty$ and $0\le C_{\mathrm e}<\infty$. There is a finite $\lambda_0=\lambda_0(c,C,C_{\mathrm e})>0$ with the following property. Let $\lambda\ge\lambda_0$, $x\ge0$, and let $h$ be a horizontal Hermitian form with $cI\le h\le CI$. Put $$q=x(1+x),\qquad G=\lambda I+xh,\qquad D=(1+2x)h-qhG^{-1}h.$$ Let $T_4,T_3$ be tensors with the Kähler symmetries and reality condition, supported respectively on components with four and with three horizontal indices, and satisfying $$\\|T_4\\|\le C_{\mathrm e}x,\qquad \\|T_3\\|\le C_{\mathrm e}\sqrt q.$$ Define $$R=-\lambda S_I-qS_h-S_V-(S_{D+V}-S_D-S_V)+T_4+T_3.$$ Then $\mathcal H_R<0$ on every real two-plane.
  - Explanation: A finite threshold on the base weight makes the curvature numerator strictly signed on every real two-plane, uniformly over all permitted base forms and error tensors, by a contradiction argument split into two parameter regimes.

          "source_id": "PM-359.1"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-peaks-bounds",
        "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $C,c>0$, independent of $D_0\\ge1$, such that $$\\label{peaks:count}\n \\frac{k}{D_0^2}\\le N_k\\le\\frac{4k}{D_0^2}$$ and, for every $\\xi\\in S$ and $u\\ge0$, $$\\label{peaks:gaussian}\n \\sum_{\\substack{p\\in\\mathcal P_k\\\\\\sqrt{k}d(\\xi,p)\\ge u}}\n       |\\langle\\xi,p\\rangle|^k\\le Ce^{-cu^2}.$$ Consequently, $$\\label{peaks:global}\n |P_k(z)|\\le C|z|^k\\quad(z\\in\\mathbb C^2),\n \\qquad\n \\int_S|P_k|\\,d\\sigma\\le\\frac{2N_k}{k+2}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-359.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

          "source_id": "PM-359.1"
        }
      ],

        "anchor": "stmt-peaks-patches",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $R_0>0$. If $D_0>\\max\\{1,2\\sqrt{2R_0}\\}$ and $k\\ge\\max\\{D_0^2,2R_0\\}$, the patches $\\mathcal U_{k,p}$ are pairwise disjoint.         "exact_quote": "Fix $1<a<2$ and put $\\alpha=\\log a$. One can choose $0<s_1<1$, smooth real functions $f,b$ on $[0,\\infty)$, and $R_0>0$ such that $$\\label{peaks:profile-negativity}\n \\alpha^2-2s_1(\\cosh\\alpha-1)<0,$$ $$\\label{peaks:profile-identities}\n \\|f\\|_\\infty<1,\\quad f=-s_1\\ \\text{on }[0,\\alpha],\\quad\n \\int_0^\\infty e^{-y}f(y)\\,dy=0,\\quad\n b'-b=f,\\quad b(0)=0.$$ Both functions vanish on a neighborhood of $[R_0,\\infty)$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-density-main",
        "exact_quote": "There is a constant $K_*>0$, depending only on the fixed profiles $f,b$ and $R_0$, with the following property. For every fixed $D_0>\\max\\{1,2\\sqrt{2R_0}\\}$ there is an integer threshold $Q_0$ such that, for every integer $Q\\ge Q_0$ and every choice of the separated sets above, the recursion <a href=\"#density:recursion\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"density:recursion\">[density:recursion]</a> has the following properties for all $j\\ge1$:*\n\n1.  *$W_j$ is smooth and strictly positive, and $W_j\\,d\\sigma$ represents holomorphic evaluation at zero. In particular it is a probability measure.*\n\n2.  *Under global phase rotation, $W_j$ has frequencies only between $-\\ell_j$ and $\\ell_j$, and its mean on every phase orbit is exactly one.*\n\n3.  *At every point of $S$, $$\\label{density:uniform-bounds}\n     \\begin{gathered}\n     c_0W_{j-1}\\le W_j\\le2W_{j-1},\\\\\n     |ZW_j|\\le K_*\\sqrt{k_j}\\,W_j,\n     \\qquad |TW_j|\\le K_*k_jW_j\n     \\end{gathered}$$ for every unit horizontal vector $Z$.*\n\n*The threshold $Q_0$ may also be enlarged to impose any fixed lower threshold on the degrees $k_j$.",
        "source_id": "PM-359.1"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-359.1"

      "exact_statement": "There is a constant $K_*>0$, depending only on the fixed profiles $f,b$ and $R_0$, with the following property.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-density-moments",
        "exact_quote": "For every stage of <a href=\"#density:recursion\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"density:recursion\">[density:recursion]</a>, the phase bandwidth of $W_j$ is at most $\\ell_j$, its orbitwise mean is one, and $$\\label{density:representing}\n \\int_S p(\\xi)W_j(\\xi)\\,d\\sigma(\\xi)=p(0)\n \\quad\\text{for every holomorphic polynomial }p.$$ Each individual correction has zero integral against every such polynomial, including the constant polynomial.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-density-orbit-growth",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $Q\\ge2$, $k_j=Q^j$, and the positive functions $W_0=1,W_1,\\ldots,W_n$, with $n\\ge1$, satisfy $$c_0W_{j-1}\\le W_j\\le2W_{j-1},\n \\qquad |TW_j|\\le K_*k_jW_j\n \\quad(1\\le j\\le n).$$ Put $$R=\\frac2{c_0},\\qquad \\beta=\\log_2R.$$ For every $p\\in S$, the function $S_p(\\theta)=W_n(e^{i\\theta}p)$ satisfies $$\\label{density:orbit-growth-bound}\n S_p(\\theta+u)\n \\le e^{K_*}R(1+k_n|u|)^\\beta S_p(\\theta),\n \\qquad |u|\\le\\pi.$$ In particular, the constants in this bound are independent of $Q,n,p$.",
        "source_id": "PM-359.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-density-orbit-growth",

          "source_id": "PM-359.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Suppose $Q\\ge2$, $k_j=Q^j$, and the positive functions $W_0=1,W_1,\\ldots,W_n$, with $n\\ge1$, satisfy $$c_0W_{j-1}\\le W_j\\le2W_{j-1},\n \\qquad |TW_j|\\le K_*k_jW_j\n \\quad(1\\le j\\le n).$$ Put $$R=\\frac2{c_0},\\qquad \\beta=\\log_2R.$$ For every $p\\in S$, the function $S_p(\\theta)=W_n(e^{i\\theta}p)$ satisfies $$\\label{density:orbit-growth-bound}\n S_p(\\theta+u)\n \\le e^{K_*}R(1+k_n|u|)^\\beta S_p(\\theta),\n \\qquad |u|\\le\\pi.$$ In particular, the constants in this bound are independent of $Q,n,p$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-density-weighted-derivative",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $A\\ge1$ and $\\beta\\ge0$. Let $q\\ge1$ be an integer, and let $s$ be a positive trigonometric polynomial of degree at most $2q$. Suppose that $$s(\\theta+u)\\le A(1+q|u|)^\\beta s(\\theta)\n \\qquad(\\theta\\in\\mathbb R,\\ |u|\\le\\pi).$$ There is a constant $C(A,\\beta)$, independent of $q,s$, such that $$|s''(\\theta)|\\le C(A,\\beta)q^2s(\\theta).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-359.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-density-weighted-derivative",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $A\\ge1$ and $\\beta\\ge0$. Let $q\\ge1$ be an integer, and let $s$ be a positive trigonometric polynomial of degree at most $2q$.           "source_id": "PM-359.1"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-density-normalized-patch",
        "exact_quote": "Write $$F=\\|f\\|_\\infty,\\quad B=\\|b\\|_\\infty,\\quad\n F_1=\\|f'\\|_\\infty,\\quad B_1=\\|b'\\|_\\infty.$$ On a degree-$k$ patch suppose $W_{j-1}>0$, $S_p>0$, and $$\\frac{e^{2y/k}S_p}{W_{j-1}}\\le2,\\qquad\n r_1=\\frac{|S_p'|}{kS_p}\\le1,\\qquad\n r_2=\\frac{|S_p''|}{k^2S_p}\\le1.$$ For the correction $A_{j,p}$ in <a href=\"#density:recursion\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"density:recursion\">[density:recursion]</a>, $$|YA_{j,p}|\\le C_2W_{j-1},\n \\quad Y=Z/\\sqrt{k}\\ \\hbox{or}\\ T/k,\n \\qquad\n C_2=2D_R\\bigl[4(F+B)+F_1+B_1\\bigr].$$ In particular, $C_2$ is independent of $K_*,Q,j,p$.",
        "source_id": "PM-359.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-density-normalized-patch",

          "source_id": "PM-359.1"

        "exact_quote": "For every fixed finite $L>0$, the potential <a href=\"#base:potential\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"base:potential\">[base:potential]</a> has uniformly bounded positive-order derivatives through order four at the origins of all centered ball charts. Consequently its complex Hessian satisfies <a href=\"#base:control\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"base:control\">[base:control]</a> and <a href=\"#base:jets\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"base:jets\">[base:jets]</a>, with $c=1$ and a finite constant $C$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-metric-complete",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\phi$ be a smooth real plurisubharmonic function on $\\mathbb B$, and let $\\lambda>0$. The domain $M_\\phi$ in <a href=\"#metric:domain\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"metric:domain\">[metric:domain]</a> is contractible, and the complex Hessian of $\\mathcal K_\\lambda=\\lambda\\psi-\\log(1-e^\\phi|w|^2)$ defines a smooth geodesically complete Kähler metric $g=2\\operatorname{Re}\\widehat g$ on $M_\\phi$. Moreover, $\\mathcal K_\\lambda$ is a smooth strictly plurisubharmonic exhaustion of $M_\\phi$.",
        "source_id": "PM-359.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-curv-tensor",
        "exact_quote": "In the frame just described, define the horizontal Hermitian form $$D=(1+2x)h-qhG^{-1}h.$$ The full curvature tensor is $$\\label{curv:tensor-identity}\n R=-\\lambda S_I-qS_h-S_V\n    -(S_{D+V}-S_D-S_V)+T_4+T_3,$$ where $T_j$ has exactly $j$ horizontal indices. Its nonzero components, up to the Kähler symmetries and conjugation, are $$\\begin{aligned}\n (T_4)_{i\\bar j k\\bar l}\n   &=-x h_{i\\bar j,k\\bar l}\n      +x^2G^{p\\bar e}h_{i\\bar e,k}h_{p\\bar j,\\bar l},\n       \\label{curv:four-horizontal-error}\\\\\n (T_3)_{i\\bar j k\\bar0}\n   &=\\sqrt q\\bigl(-h_{i\\bar j,k}\n           +xG^{p\\bar e}h_{i\\bar e,k}h_{p\\bar j}\\bigr).\n       \\label{curv:three-horizontal-error}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Measured by the Euclidean component norms in this frame, uniformly for $\\lambda\\ge1$, $$\\label{curv:component-errors}\n \\|T_4\\|\\le C_4x,\\qquad \\|T_3\\|\\le C_3\\sqrt q.$$ If $\\lambda$ is at least the uniform upper bound for the eigenvalues of $h$ at centered-chart origins, then $$\\label{curv:D-bounds}\n (1+x)h\\le D\\le(1+2x)h.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-359.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **completeness and sectional curvature convention** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Here and throughout, sectional curvature refers to the underlying real Riemannian metric, and completeness means geodesic completeness.
- **bounded holomorphic map** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A bounded holomorphic map is a holomorphic map with bounded image in its Euclidean target.
- **Hartogs domain** ([source](#metric:domain)): For any smooth real $\phi$ on $\mathbb B$, define its Hartogs domain by $$\label{metric:domain} M_\phi=\{(z,w)\in\mathbb B\times\mathbb C:t(z,w)<1\}, \qquad t(z,w)=e^{\phi(z)}\|w\|^2.$$
- **real metric convention** ([source](#metric:potential)): Our real metric convention is $g_\lambda=2\operatorname{Re}\widehat g_\lambda$: if a real vector $X$ has $(1,0)$ part $u$, then $g_\lambda(X,X)=2\widehat g_\lambda(u,u)$.
- **phase bandwidth and orbitwise mean** ([source](#sec:densities)): A function has phase bandwidth at most $\ell$ if, on every orbit $\omega\mapsto e^{i\omega}\xi$, it is a trigonometric polynomial with frequencies in $[-\ell,\ell]$.
- **orbitwise mean** ([source](#sec:densities)): Its orbitwise mean is the constant coefficient of this polynomial.
- **horizontal vector** ([source](#sec:densities)): A horizontal vector $Z\in T_\xi S$ will always mean a *Euclidean unit* real tangent vector satisfying the complex equation $\langle Z,\xi\rangle=0$.
- **Gram quantities of a plane** ([source](#pm-h-22)): Thus $a_P$ is the determinant of the real Gram matrix for $\operatorname{Re}P$, $b_P$ is the imaginary Hermitian pairing, and $m_P$ is the cross term in that determinant after adding $V$.

      "exact_definition": "Our real metric convention is $g_\\lambda=2\\operatorname{Re}\\widehat g_\\lambda$: if a real vector $X$ has $(1,0)$ part $u$, then $g_\\lambda(X,X)=2\\widehat g_\\lambda(u,u)$.",
      "interpretation": "Relates the Hermitian Hessian of the potential to the real Riemannian metric used for sectional curvature.",
      "kind": "notation",
      "name": "real metric convention",
      "scope": "Hartogs metric and curvature computations."
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "sec:densities",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "sec:densities",
          "exact_quote": "A function has phase bandwidth at most $\\ell$ if, on every orbit $\\omega\\mapsto e^{i\\omega}\\xi$, it is a trigonometric polynomial with frequencies in $[-\\ell,\\ell]$.",
          "source_id": "PM-359.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "A function has phase bandwidth at most $\\ell$ if, on every orbit $\\omega\\mapsto e^{i\\omega}\\xi$, it is a trigonometric polynomial with frequencies in $[-\\ell,\\ell]$.",
      "interpretation": "Measures how high the phase frequencies of a density on the sphere can be along rotation orbits.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "phase bandwidth and orbitwise mean",
      "scope": "Densities on the unit sphere in complex dimension two, used in moment and averaging lemmas."
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "sec:densities",

          "anchor": "sec:densities",
          "exact_quote": "Its orbitwise mean is the constant coefficient of this polynomial.",
          "source_id": "PM-359.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "Its orbitwise mean is the constant coefficient of this polynomial.",
      "interpretation": "The zero-frequency part of a function along each phase orbit; kept equal to one for the densities.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "orbitwise mean",

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-curvature-pinch | real sectional curvature of the constructed metric | ≤ | $-B\le K_g(\sigma)\le -A<0$ | finite constants with A at most B |
| q-peak-count | number of separated line centers at a given degree | ≤ | $\frac{k}{D_0^2}\le N_k\le\frac{4k}{D_0^2}$ | spacing parameter at least one and degree at least its square |
| q-peak-l1 | integral of the modulus of the peak polynomial over the sphere | ≤ | $\int_S\|P_k\|\,d\sigma\le\frac{2N_k}{k+2}$ | all admissible degrees and spacings |
| q-density-ratio | ratio of consecutive densities | ≤ | $c_0W_{j-1}\le W_j\le2W_{j-1}$ | degree ratio above the threshold of the density theorem |
| q-one-scale-negative | average of the test function against its density | ≤ | $\int_S U_{k_j}(r\xi)W_j(\xi)\,d\sigma\le-d_2$ | every stage, constants independent of the stage and of further increases of the degree ratio |
| q-amplitude-threshold | amplitude of the base potential series | > | $L>\frac{5\log Q}{d_3}$ | degree ratio above the later-density threshold |
| q-jacobian-determinant | modulus of the Jacobian determinant on the zero section | ≤ | $\|d(z)\|\le6B^3\delta^{-2}e^{\phi(z)/2}$ | base points, with delta the distance of the base point to the boundary and B the bound of the map |
| q-gradient-base | squared gradient norm of the base exhaustion function | ≤ | $\|\nabla\psi\|_g^2\le\frac{2\|z\|^2}{\lambda}\le\frac2\lambda$ | positive base weight |
| q-vertical-curvature | normalized vertical curvature component | = | $R_{0\bar00\bar0}=-2$ | all fiber values |
| q-mixed-gram | mixed Gram cross term | ≥ | $m_P\ge2\sqrt{a_Pa_V}$ | all such planes |
| q-error-four-horizontal | component norm of the four-horizontal error tensor | ≤ | $\\|T_4\\|\le C_4x$ | base weight at least one, constants from the chart bounds |
| q-error-three-horizontal | component norm of the three-horizontal error tensor | ≤ | $\\|T_3\\|\le C_3\sqrt q$ | base weight at least one, constants from the chart bounds |
| q-auxiliary-form-sandwich | horizontal auxiliary Hermitian form | ≤ | $(1+x)h\le D\le(1+2x)h$ | base weight at least the uniform upper bound of the eigenvalues of the base form |
| q-asymptotic-curvature | sectional curvature at large fiber parameter | ≤ | $-3\le K_g\le-1/4\qquad(x\ge X_\lambda)$ | fiber parameter beyond a sufficiently large threshold |
| q-radii-sequence | radii approaching the boundary | = | $r_N=1-\frac{\eta}{k_N}$ | all stage indices |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-mean-criterion** [claim](#stmt-peaks-mean-criterion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-peaks-mean-criterion): Taylor polynomials converge uniformly on a smaller sphere, so representing identities give the mean of a holomorphic real part; integrating the assumed inequality contradicts the limit. -> Obstruction criterion for holomorphic real parts below a function.
- **step-peak-bounds** [claim](#stmt-peaks-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-peaks-bounds): Covering and packing by projective caps give the count; a local count with Gaussian decay of powers gives the tail estimate and the global bounds. -> Count, Gaussian tail and integral bounds for the peak polynomial.
- **step-patch-estimates** [claim](#stmt-peaks-patches) / [proof](#proof-stmt-peaks-patches): Triangle inequality for the projective metric gives disjointness and single-peak approximation; the Gaussian tail bounds the remaining peaks and the off-patch region. -> Disjoint patches with explicit measure and single-peak control.
- **step-radial-profiles** [claim](#stmt-peaks-profiles) / [proof](#proof-stmt-peaks-profiles): Choose the plateau value from the strict hyperbolic inequality, glue a cutoff and a compensating bump to get the weighted cancellation, and integrate to obtain the companion function. -> Profiles with plateau, cancellation and negativity.
- **step-density-smoothness** [claim](#stmt-density-smooth) / [proof](#proof-stmt-density-smooth): Induction using smooth coordinates along the central circle and vanishing of the profiles in a collar of the patch boundary. -> Smooth densities at every stage.
- **step-moment-preservation** [claim](#stmt-density-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-density-moments): Fourier expansion along phase orbits, positivity of the shifted frequencies and exact radial integration by parts on each patch. -> Bandwidth, orbit mean and holomorphic moments of every density.
- **step-orbit-growth** [claim](#stmt-density-orbit-growth) / [proof](#proof-stmt-density-orbit-growth): Telescoping the stagewise comparisons up to the largest scale below the displacement and logarithmic differentiation at that scale. -> Polynomial growth bound along phase orbits.
- **step-weighted-derivative** [claim](#stmt-density-weighted-derivative) / [proof](#proof-stmt-density-weighted-derivative): Represent the second derivative as a convolution with a smoothed kernel, prove kernel decay by finite differences, and integrate against the growth bound. -> Second-derivative bound relative to the value.
- **step-normalized-correction** [claim](#stmt-density-normalized-patch) / [proof](#proof-stmt-density-normalized-patch): Differentiate the recursion on a patch and bound each term using the geometric direction estimates and the normalized ratios. -> Correction derivative bound with a constant fixed by the profile data.
- **step-uniform-densities** [claim](#stmt-density-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-density-normalized-patch-2): Fix the derivative constant, then a degree-ratio threshold, and run an induction over stages combining central comparison, orbit growth, the weighted derivative bound and the normalized correction bound. -> Positive representing densities with uniform derivative bounds.
- **step-exact-averaging** [claim](#stmt-tests-averaging) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tests-averaging): Phase averaging with the frequency support of the test function and of the density increments. -> Averaging identity with a patchwise cosine term.
- **step-negative-average-one-scale** [claim](#stmt-tests-negative) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tests-negative): Compute the single-peak model integral using logarithm series, compare with the patch and off-patch contributions, and choose the regularization, radius window and spacing in order. -> Uniformly negative average at one scale.
- **step-negative-average-later** [claim](#stmt-tests-later) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tests-later): Fourier expansion of the test function along orbits, truncation by frequency and phase orthogonality against the later increments. -> Negative average against all later densities.
- **step-smooth-convergence** [claim](#stmt-base-convergence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-base-convergence): Cauchy estimates in polydisks and exponential smallness of derivatives of the peak polynomials, then termwise differentiation. -> Smooth potential with Hessian at least the ball metric.
- **step-no-minorant** [claim](#stmt-base-minorant) / [proof](#proof-stmt-base-minorant): Evaluate at radii approaching the boundary, split into early and late modes, bound the tail, and apply the representing-mean criterion. -> No holomorphic real part below the potential plus the allowance.
- **step-chart-estimates** [claim](#stmt-base-chart-estimates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-base-chart-estimates): Split degrees into low and high regimes relative to the boundary distance, use Cauchy estimates in a fixed polydisk, and sum geometric series. -> Uniform jet bounds through order four at chart origins.
- **step-controlled-potential** [claim](#stmt-base-existence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-base-chart-estimates-2): Fix the profiles, regularization, spacing, degree ratio and then the amplitude, and assemble the convergence, obstruction and chart bounds. -> The controlled potential of the proposition.
- **step-hartogs-complete** [claim](#stmt-metric-complete) / [proof](#proof-stmt-metric-complete): Explicit diffeomorphism to a product for contractibility, a global coframe for positivity, gradient bounds for compact containment of metric balls, and extension of geodesics. -> Complete Kähler metric and strictly plurisubharmonic exhaustion.
- **step-curvature-tensor** [claim](#stmt-curv-tensor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-curv-tensor): Chain rule for the radial function in a holomorphic gauge, derivative tables and substitution into the curvature formula. -> Curvature tensor with explicit leading terms and error tensors.
- **step-real-plane-curvature** [claim](#stmt-curv-real-plane) / [proof](#proof-stmt-curv-real-plane): Expand the real curvature numerator on the complex parts of the vectors and divide by the real squared area. -> Sectional curvature as a ratio with Gram determinant denominator.
- **step-gram-inequalities** [claim](#stmt-curv-gram) / [proof](#proof-stmt-curv-gram): Cauchy–Schwarz for Hermitian forms and a trace versus determinant inequality for real Gram matrices. -> Gram inequalities with equality criterion.
- **step-contracted-errors** [claim](#stmt-curv-errors) / [proof](#proof-stmt-curv-errors): Cauchy–Schwarz on the contraction with the wedge matrix, using the block structure of the error tensors. -> Bounds on the contracted error terms.
- **step-mixed-plane-algebra** [claim](#stmt-pinch-algebra) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pinch-algebra): Bound the imaginary pairing through norms of complex combinations, combine with the mixed Gram inequality to complete a square, and analyze equality. -> Nonnegativity of the leading expression with a characterization of zeros.
- **step-uniform-negativity** [claim](#stmt-pinch-positivity) / [proof](#pinch:leading-numerator): Contradiction along a sequence of large weights, with one regime handled by absorption and the other by rescaling, compactness, the algebraic lemma and improved error control on limiting planes. -> A finite weight threshold giving strict signed curvature numerator.
- **step-metric-transfer** [claim](#stmt-metric-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pinch-positivity-2): Compactness on bounded fiber ranges and a uniform limiting tensor at the fiber boundary give both curvature bounds at a fixed weight. -> Complete metric with two finite negative curvature bounds.
- **step-jacobian-obstruction** [claim](#stmt-map-obstruction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-map-obstruction): Cauchy estimates on base and fiber discs along the zero section bound the Jacobian, and a holomorphic logarithm of the nonvanishing determinant gives a forbidden holomorphic real part. -> No bounded map with nowhere-vanishing Jacobian.
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-main-theorem) / [proof](#proof-stmt-map-obstruction-2): Choose the potential, then the weight, and apply the Jacobian obstruction, noting that a biholomorphism to a bounded domain would be such a map. -> The counterexample to the uniformization question.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-pinched-no-bounded-coordinates**: The constructed contractible domain in complex dimension three carries a complete Kähler metric whose real sectional curvatures all lie between two finite negative constants. No bounded holomorphic map to the three-dimensional complex space has a nowhere-vanishing Jacobian determinant, so the domain is not biholomorphic to a bounded domain.
- **inv-metric-transfer-threshold**: For a base potential with controlled Hessian bounds and derivative bounds in centered ball charts, a finite threshold for the base weight exists, depending only on the two Hessian bounds. For every weight above it, the Hartogs domain is contractible, the metric is smooth and geodesically complete, and the real sectional curvature is pinched between two negative constants.
- **inv-representing-mean-obstruction**: If averages of a continuous function over spheres of radii below one, taken against probabilities that represent holomorphic evaluation at the origin, tend to minus infinity, then no holomorphic real part can lie below that function plus a constant.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-no-bounded-functions-at-all**: Cite the result to claim that a complete negatively pinched Kähler manifold has no nonconstant bounded holomorphic functions. -> The example still carries two nonconstant bounded holomorphic functions from its base coordinates; only a full bounded coordinate triple with nowhere-vanishing Jacobian is excluded.
- **anti-other-dimensions**: Transport the counterexample and its parameters unchanged to other complex dimensions. -> The construction couples a two-dimensional complex ball base with a one-dimensional fiber disc, and its packing and sphere estimates use that specific geometry.
- **anti-explicit-constants**: Instantiate numerical code or calibrated pinching constants from the thresholds in the density construction. -> The constants are obtained only existentially through a sequence of choices, so no explicit value is available to plug in.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The paper proves existence of a domain, a potential and a metric through non-explicit choices of profiles, spacing, degrees, amplitude and thresholds, with compactness and contradiction arguments; no finite procedure outputs an object, so no executable reference or independent oracle can be given.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves an existence statement about a complex domain and a metric. It specifies no program, interface or data format that software could implement. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The Hartogs-type domain is an analytic object. The paper gives no discretisation, time stepping or simulation rule, so there is nothing to render or simulate faithfully. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No theorem is turned into a checkable procedure. The result could at most serve as a negative fixture for a tool, and that fixture is not a construction in the paper. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The density recursion is a recursive definition of smooth functions on a sphere. Its threshold parameters are existential and it has no finite step bound, so it is not a runnable algorithm with a complexity. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The separated sets of lines and the patches are analytic packing devices used in estimates. They are not organised as a data structure with operations or costs. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the paper concerns arithmetic kernels, precision or parallel layout; the estimates are asymptotic analytic bounds. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no hardness assumption, adversary model or protocol. Its topic is curvature and holomorphic coordinates. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no learning problem, loss function or generalisation statement. The plurisubharmonic potentials are not trained or fitted. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No objective is minimised and no feasibility problem is posed. The parameters are chosen only to make estimates hold, not to optimise a quantity. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper contains no processes, messages, failures or consensus. Nothing in it maps to a distributed protocol. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The only plausible bridge is a negative fixture for geometry software or a formal-proof target for the main statement, which would be inspirational rather than a construction taken from the paper. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-complex-dimension-three** (hypothesis): The counterexample is a domain in complex dimension three, built from a two-dimensional complex ball base and a one-dimensional disc fibre. The packing, sphere and curvature estimates use this specific geometry. Blocked use: ['Transporting the counterexample or its parameters unchanged to other complex dimensions']
- **limit-bounded-map-obstruction-only** (counterexample_scope): Only a bounded holomorphic map to complex three-space with nowhere-vanishing Jacobian is excluded. The example keeps two nonconstant bounded holomorphic functions, namely the base coordinates. Blocked use: ['Claiming that pinched negatively curved complete Kähler manifolds have only constant bounded holomorphic functions']
- **limit-lambda-threshold-existential** (nonconstructive): The metric transfer gives a finite threshold for the base weight, but the threshold is obtained through a contradiction and compactness argument and is not an explicit number. Blocked use: ['Computing a numerical pinching constant or a numerical weight from the paper', 'Choosing a weight below the threshold and expecting negative pinching']
- **limit-chart-control-hypothesis** (hypothesis): The metric transfer needs uniform two-sided Hessian control and bounds on the first and second derivatives of the Hessian components in every centered ball chart. Without these bounds the curvature error tensors are not controlled. Blocked use: ['Applying the metric transfer to a base potential lacking uniform chart jets']
- **limit-density-thresholds** (nonconstructive): The positive representing densities exist only for a degree ratio above a threshold, and the derivative constant is fixed before that threshold. Both depend on fixed profile data chosen non-explicitly. Blocked use: ['Running the density recursion with a guessed small degree ratio', 'Treating the derivative constants as computable parameters']
- **limit-amplitude-condition** (hypothesis): Excluding a holomorphic real part below the potential requires the amplitude to exceed a bound involving the degree ratio and the one-scale negativity constant. Blocked use: ['Using a small amplitude and still asserting the absence of a pluriharmonic minorant']
- **limit-representing-not-jensen** (formal_scope): The probabilities only represent holomorphic evaluation at the origin. They need not satisfy the logarithmic mean inequality for holomorphic functions, so Jensen-type arguments cannot be substituted. Blocked use: ['Replacing the representing measures by Jensen measures in the obstruction argument']
- **limit-stein-not-obstructed** (edge_case): The domain is Stein, so the obstruction concerns bounded holomorphic coordinates, not holomorphic convexity. Blocked use: ['Reading the result as a failure of holomorphic convexity of the example']

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 359 (`lean/docs/359.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [A negatively pinched Kähler threefold without bounded holomorphic coordinates](../../preprints/A-negatively-pinched-Kahler-threefold-without-bounded-holomorphic-coordinates-September-25-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalized result constructs a nonempty contractible complex threefold with a complete Kähler metric whose real sectional curvatures lie between two fixed negative constants. Nevertheless, it has no system of bounded holomorphic coordinates and is not biholomorphic to a bounded domain. The metric is obtained from an explicitly controlled potential. This is the negatively pinched construction; the different companion with only one-sided curvature control is separate.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section

open Set Filter Topology
open scoped ContDiff

namespace PinchedHartogs

abbrev Base := EuclideanSpace ℂ (Fin 2)
abbrev Ambient := Base × ℂ
abbrev Target := Fin 3 → ℂ

def ball : Set Base := {z | ‖z‖ < 1}

def hartogs (φ : Base → ℝ) : Set Ambient :=
  {p | p.1 ∈ ball ∧ Real.exp (φ p.1) * ‖p.2‖ ^ 2 < 1}

def noMinorant (φ : Base → ℝ) : Prop :=
  ¬ ∃ (H : Base → ℂ) (C : ℝ), AnalyticOnNhd ℂ H ball ∧
    ∀ z ∈ ball, (H z).re ≤ C + 4 * Real.log (1 / (1 - ‖z‖)) + φ z

def coordVector (j : Fin 3) : Ambient :=
  ![(EuclideanSpace.single 0 1, 0), (EuclideanSpace.single 1 1, 0), (0, 1)] j

def jacobian (F : Ambient → Target) (p : Ambient) : Matrix (Fin 3) (Fin 3) ℂ :=
  fun i j ↦ (fderiv ℂ F p (coordVector j)) i

open scoped Matrix.Norms.Elementwise

abbrev CMatrix := Matrix (Fin 3) (Fin 3) ℂ
abbrev CurvatureTensor := Fin 3 → Fin 3 → Fin 3 → Fin 3 → ℂ

def coordinates (u : Ambient) : Fin 3 → ℂ := ![u.1 0, u.1 1, u.2]

def dz (f : Ambient → ℂ) (p : Ambient) (j : Fin 3) : ℂ :=
  (fderiv ℝ f p (coordVector j) - Complex.I * fderiv ℝ f p (Complex.I • coordVector j)) / 2

def dbar (f : Ambient → ℂ) (p : Ambient) (j : Fin 3) : ℂ :=
  (fderiv ℝ f p (coordVector j) + Complex.I * fderiv ℝ f p (Complex.I • coordVector j)) / 2

def hermitianValue (h : CMatrix) (u v : Ambient) : ℂ :=
  ∑ i, ∑ j, h i j * coordinates u i * star (coordinates v j)

def realArea (h : CMatrix) (u v : Ambient) : ℝ :=
  (hermitianValue h u u).re * (hermitianValue h v v).re - (hermitianValue h u v).re ^ 2

def tensorValue (R : CurvatureTensor) (u v w y : Ambient) : ℂ :=
  ∑ a, ∑ b, ∑ c, ∑ d,
    R a b c d * coordinates u a * star (coordinates v b) * coordinates w c * star (coordinates y d)

def curvature (g : Ambient → CMatrix) (p : Ambient) : CurvatureTensor := fun a b c d ↦
  -dbar (fun q ↦ dz (fun r ↦ g r c d) q a) p b +
    ∑ e, ∑ k, (g p)⁻¹ e k * dz (fun q ↦ g q c e) p a * dbar (fun q ↦ g q k d) p b

def sectional (g : Ambient → CMatrix) (p u v : Ambient) : ℝ :=
  ((tensorValue (curvature g p) u u v v).re - (tensorValue (curvature g p) u v u v).re) /
    (2 * realArea (g p) u v)

def IsKahlerMetric (M : Set Ambient) (g : Ambient → CMatrix) : Prop :=
  ContDiffOn ℝ ∞ g M ∧
  (∀ p ∈ M, ∀ i j, g p i j = star (g p j i)) ∧
  (∀ p ∈ M, ∀ u : Ambient, u ≠ 0 → 0 < (hermitianValue (g p) u u).re) ∧
  (∀ p ∈ M, ∀ i j k, dz (fun q ↦ g q k j) p i = dz (fun q ↦ g q i j) p k)

def christoffel (g : Ambient → CMatrix) (p : Ambient) (k a c : Fin 3) : ℂ :=
  ∑ e, (g p)⁻¹ e k * dz (fun q ↦ g q c e) p a

def GeodesicallyComplete (M : Set Ambient) (g : Ambient → CMatrix) : Prop :=
  ∀ p ∈ M, ∀ v : Ambient, ∃ γ : ℝ → Ambient,
    ContDiff ℝ ∞ γ ∧ (∀ t, γ t ∈ M) ∧ γ 0 = p ∧ deriv γ 0 = v ∧
      ∀ t k, coordinates (deriv (deriv γ) t) k +
        ∑ a, ∑ c, christoffel g (γ t) k a c * coordinates (deriv γ t) a *
          coordinates (deriv γ t) c = 0

def IsNegativelyPinched (M : Set Ambient) (g : Ambient → CMatrix) (A B : ℝ) : Prop :=
  0 < A ∧ A ≤ B ∧ ∀ p ∈ M, ∀ u v : Ambient, LinearIndependent ℝ ![u, v] →
    -B ≤ sectional g p u v ∧ sectional g p u v ≤ -A

def NoBoundedCoordinates (M : Set Ambient) : Prop :=
  ∀ F : Ambient → Target, AnalyticOnNhd ℂ F M →
    (∃ B : ℝ, ∀ p ∈ M, ∀ i, ‖F p i‖ ≤ B) →
      ¬ ∀ p ∈ M, (jacobian F p).det ≠ 0

def BiholomorphicToBoundedDomain (M : Set Ambient) : Prop :=
  ∃ N : Set Ambient, IsOpen N ∧ Bornology.IsBounded N ∧
    ∃ F G : Ambient → Ambient, AnalyticOnNhd ℂ F M ∧ AnalyticOnNhd ℂ G N ∧
      MapsTo F M N ∧ MapsTo G N M ∧ (∀ p ∈ M, G (F p) = p) ∧ (∀ q ∈ N, F (G q) = q)

def MainTheorem : Prop :=
  ∃ (M : Set Ambient) (g : Ambient → CMatrix) (A B : ℝ),
    IsOpen M ∧ M.Nonempty ∧ ContractibleSpace M ∧ IsKahlerMetric M g ∧
      GeodesicallyComplete M g ∧ IsNegativelyPinched M g A B ∧
      NoBoundedCoordinates M ∧ ¬ BiholomorphicToBoundedDomain M

def psi (z : Base) : ℝ := -Real.log (1 - ‖z‖ ^ 2)
def metricPotential (φ : Base → ℝ) (lam : ℝ) (p : Ambient) : ℝ :=
  lam * psi p.1 - Real.log (1 - Real.exp (φ p.1) * ‖p.2‖ ^ 2)

def complexHessian (f : Ambient → ℝ) (p : Ambient) : CMatrix :=
  fun i j ↦ dz (fun q ↦ dbar (fun r ↦ (f r : ℂ)) q j) p i

def hartogsMetric (φ : Base → ℝ) (lam : ℝ) : Ambient → CMatrix :=
  complexHessian (metricPotential φ lam)

def dzBase (f : Base → ℂ) (z : Base) (i : Fin 2) : ℂ :=
  (fderiv ℝ f z (EuclideanSpace.single i 1) -
    Complex.I * fderiv ℝ f z (Complex.I • EuclideanSpace.single i 1)) / 2

def dbarBase (f : Base → ℂ) (z : Base) (i : Fin 2) : ℂ :=
  (fderiv ℝ f z (EuclideanSpace.single i 1) +
    Complex.I * fderiv ℝ f z (Complex.I • EuclideanSpace.single i 1)) / 2

def baseHessian (φ : Base → ℝ) (z : Base) : Matrix (Fin 2) (Fin 2) ℂ :=
  fun i j ↦ dzBase (fun q ↦ dbarBase (fun r ↦ (φ r : ℂ)) q j) z i

def centeredChart (r : ℝ) (U : Base ≃ₗᵢ[ℂ] Base) (ζ : Base) : Base :=
  U (WithLp.toLp 2 ![((r : ℂ) + ζ 0) / (1 + (r : ℂ) * ζ 0),
    ((Real.sqrt (1 - r ^ 2) : ℝ) : ℂ) * ζ 1 / (1 + (r : ℂ) * ζ 0)])

def ChartControl (φ : Base → ℝ) (c C : ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∀ r : ℝ, 0 ≤ r → r < 1 → ∀ U : Base ≃ₗᵢ[ℂ] Base, ∀ v : Base,
    c * ‖v‖ ^ 2 ≤ (∑ i, ∑ j, baseHessian (φ ∘ centeredChart r U) 0 i j * v i * star (v j)).re ∧
    (∑ i, ∑ j, baseHessian (φ ∘ centeredChart r U) 0 i j * v i * star (v j)).re ≤ C * ‖v‖ ^ 2

def ChartJets (φ : Base → ℝ) (C : ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∀ r : ℝ, 0 ≤ r → r < 1 → ∀ U : Base ≃ₗᵢ[ℂ] Base, ∀ i j k l : Fin 2,
    ‖dzBase (fun z ↦ baseHessian (φ ∘ centeredChart r U) z i j) 0 k‖ +
    ‖dbarBase (fun z ↦ baseHessian (φ ∘ centeredChart r U) z i j) 0 l‖ +
    ‖dzBase (fun z ↦ dbarBase (fun q ↦ baseHessian (φ ∘ centeredChart r U) q i j) z l) 0 k‖ ≤ C

def ControlledPotential : Prop :=
  ∃ φ : Base → ℝ, ∃ C : ℝ, ContDiffOn ℝ ∞ φ ball ∧
    ChartControl φ 1 C ∧ ChartJets φ C ∧ noMinorant φ

def MetricTransfer : Prop :=
  ∀ c C : ℝ, 0 < c → c ≤ C → ∃ lam0 : ℝ, 0 < lam0 ∧
    ∀ φ : Base → ℝ, ContDiffOn ℝ ∞ φ ball → ChartControl φ c C → ChartJets φ C →
      ∀ lam : ℝ, lam0 ≤ lam → ContractibleSpace (hartogs φ) ∧
        IsKahlerMetric (hartogs φ) (hartogsMetric φ lam) ∧
        GeodesicallyComplete (hartogs φ) (hartogsMetric φ lam) ∧
        ∃ A B : ℝ, IsNegativelyPinched (hartogs φ) (hartogsMetric φ lam) A B

theorem main_theorem : MainTheorem := by
  sorry

theorem controlled_potential : ControlledPotential := by
  sorry

theorem metric_transfer : MetricTransfer := by
  sorry

end PinchedHartogs

end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We construct a contractible domain in complex dimension three with a complete Kähler metric whose real sectional curvatures lie between two finite negative constants. It admits no bounded holomorphic map to ℂ<sup>3</sup> with nowhere-vanishing Jacobian and is therefore not biholomorphic to a bounded domain. This gives a negative answer to the negatively pinched Kähler uniformization question.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

A bounded domain in $\mathbb C^n$ has bounded holomorphic coordinate functions whose differentials are independent everywhere. The negatively curved uniformization problem asks whether curvature and completeness can force a complex manifold to possess such coordinates. In complex dimension one, a complete simply connected surface whose curvature is bounded above by a negative constant is biholomorphic to the disk. In higher dimension, the existence of bounded holomorphic coordinates is a separate global analytic requirement.

An early formulation appears in H. Wu’s work on normal families (H. Wu 1967, 195, question (1)) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[WuNormal1967](#ref-WuNormal1967)]. He asks whether a complete simply connected Kähler manifold with nonpositive real sectional curvature and holomorphic sectional curvature bounded above by a negative constant is biholomorphic to a bounded domain. Wu and Yau state the two-sided real-sectional version with a bounded pseudoconvex domain as conclusion (D. Wu and Yau 2019, Conjecture 4.3, p. 18) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[WuYauSurvey2019](#ref-WuYauSurvey2019)]: if a simply connected complete Kähler manifold has every real sectional curvature between two negative constants, must it be biholomorphic to a bounded domain in its complex dimension?

The unit ball is already too restrictive a candidate in higher dimension. Mostow and Siu constructed compact negatively curved Kähler surfaces not covered by the ball (Mostow and Siu 1980) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[MostowSiu1980](#ref-MostowSiu1980)]. Deraux reviews their construction and obtains three-dimensional examples (Deraux 2005) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Deraux2005](#ref-Deraux2005)]. Their lifted metrics on the universal covers have two-sided negative sectional bounds by compactness. These non-ball results, however, do not exclude a biholomorphism to some other bounded domain; that is the stronger obstruction sought here.

Here and throughout, sectional curvature refers to the underlying real Riemannian metric, and completeness means geodesic completeness. A bounded holomorphic map is a holomorphic map with bounded image in its Euclidean target.

<a id="stmt-main-theorem"></a>
<div id="main:theorem" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *There exist a contractible domain $M\subset\mathbb C^3$, a smooth complete Kähler metric $g$ on $M$, and finite constants $0<A\le B$ such that $$-B\le K_g(\sigma)\le -A<0$$ for every real two-plane $\sigma\subset T_pM$ and every $p\in M$. No bounded holomorphic map $F:M\to\mathbb C^3$ has a nowhere-vanishing Jacobian determinant. In particular, $M$ is not biholomorphic to a bounded domain in $\mathbb C^3$.*

</div>

Theorem <a href="#main:theorem" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:theorem">1</a> gives a negative answer to the stated uniformization question, and its curvature hypotheses also place the example within Wu’s earlier question. The example has two bounded nonconstant holomorphic functions, supplied by its base coordinates. Its analytic obstruction concerns a fixed triple of bounded functions with independent differentials at every point. The domain is nevertheless Stein: the construction supplies a smooth strictly plurisubharmonic exhaustion (Lemma <a href="#metric:complete" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="metric:complete">21</a>), so Grauert’s solution of the Levi problem applies (Grauert 1958, Theorem 2 and Section 3.4) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[GrauertLevi1958](#ref-GrauertLevi1958)]. Thus the obstruction is to bounded holomorphic coordinates, not to holomorphic convexity.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Intrinsic metrics and bounded functions

Two-sided negative real sectional curvature has strong consequences for intrinsic complex geometry. Greene and Wu established the lower comparison for the Bergman metric (Greene and Wu 1979) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[GreeneWu1979](#ref-GreeneWu1979)]; Wu and Yau recall that result and prove the complementary upper bound (D. Wu and Yau 2020, Theorems 4 and 6) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[WuYauInvariant2020](#ref-WuYauInvariant2020)]. Under completeness and simple connectivity, Wu and Yau then compare the Bergman, Kobayashi–Royden, complete negative Kähler–Einstein, and original metrics (D. Wu and Yau 2020, Corollary 7) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[WuYauInvariant2020](#ref-WuYauInvariant2020)]. The Bergman metric uses square-integrable holomorphic top-degree forms. These comparisons do not supply a fixed tuple of bounded holomorphic functions with independent differentials everywhere. Applied to the example in Theorem <a href="#main:theorem" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:theorem">1</a>, they show that all these intrinsic metrics can be complete and mutually comparable while no such tuple exists.

The existence of even one nonconstant bounded holomorphic function is a related question with a different conclusion. The companion paper (OpenAI 2026, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[OpenAIOneSided2026](#ref-OpenAIOneSided2026)] constructs, in a sufficiently large fixed finite complex dimension, a domain diffeomorphic to Euclidean space with a complete Kähler metric of real sectional curvature at most $-1$ and only constant bounded holomorphic functions. Its curvature is unbounded below. Here both curvature bounds are finite and uniform, while the two bounded base coordinates remain. The two constructions and their proofs are independent.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Construction and proof strategy

We construct $M$ as a family of discs over the unit ball $\mathbb B=\{z\in\mathbb C^2:|z|<1\}$. A smooth real function $\phi$ on $\mathbb B$ determines $$M_\phi=\{(z,w)\in\mathbb B\times\mathbb C:e^{\phi(z)}|w|^2<1\}.$$ Thus the disc above $z$ has radius $e^{-\phi(z)/2}$. Put $\psi(z)=-\log(1-|z|^2)$. Our metric is the complex Hessian of $$\lambda\psi(z)-\log\bigl(1-e^{\phi(z)}|w|^2\bigr),$$ with the real-metric convention specified in Section <a href="#sec:reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:reduction">2</a>. This is a specialization of Calabi’s construction from a base potential and a radial function of a Hermitian fiber norm (Calabi 1979, sec. 3, equations (3.1)–(3.2)) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Calabi1979](#ref-Calabi1979)]. The momentum formulation of Hwang and Singer develops this framework for general base and bundle data (Hwang and Singer 2002) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[HwangSinger2002](#ref-HwangSinger2002)].

For the logarithmic radial potential used here, Hao, Chen, and Wang prove holomorphic and real sectional pinching results when the bundle curvature determines the base metric (Hao, Chen, and Wang 2025, Theorems 1 and 2) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[HaoChenWang2025](#ref-HaoChenWang2025)]. Hao, Chen, Wang, and Zhang obtain related results for complete Kähler–Einstein disk-bundle metrics under Einstein and curvature hypotheses on the base (Hao et al. 2026, secs. 4.2–4.3) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[HaoChenWangZhang2026](#ref-HaoChenWangZhang2026)]. The estimate needed here concerns all real two-planes when the base form $\lambda\partial\bar\partial\psi$ and the fiber-weight curvature $\partial\bar\partial\phi$ are independent. We assume upper and lower bounds for the latter form and bounds for the component derivatives needed for curvature, expressed in centered ball coordinates. No curvature sign for the metric $\partial\bar\partial\phi$ is assumed. We derive and estimate the full curvature tensor under precisely these hypotheses.

The analytic task is to choose $\phi$ so that it has these derivative bounds but cannot dominate a holomorphic real part, even after adding the logarithmic boundary allowance forced by Cauchy’s estimate for a Jacobian. To do this, we construct radii $r_N\uparrow1$ and positive probabilities $\mu_N$ on the unit sphere $S\subset\mathbb C^2$ such that $$\int_S\left(\phi(r_N\xi)+4\log\frac1{1-r_N}\right)
                  \,d\mu_N(\xi)\longrightarrow-\infty,$$ while $\int_S\operatorname{Re}H(r_N\xi)\,d\mu_N(\xi)
=\operatorname{Re}H(0)$ for every holomorphic $H$ on $\mathbb B$. The two averages exclude any holomorphic real part bounded above by the tested function plus a constant. The probabilities preserve holomorphic evaluation at the origin, but such measures need not preserve the logarithmic mean inequality for holomorphic functions: Hedenmalm gives an explicit example of this distinction (Hedenmalm 1989, Theorem 3.7) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Hedenmalm1989](#ref-Hedenmalm1989)].

The construction proceeds at degrees $k_j=Q^j$ for one fixed large integer $Q$. At each degree we sum homogeneous peaks centered on a separated set of complex lines. The projective packing and power-sum estimate have a direct antecedent in Ryll and Wojtaszczyk (Ryll and Wojtaszczyk 1983, sec. 2) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[RyllWojtaszczyk1983](#ref-RyllWojtaszczyk1983)]. A radial correction around each peak annihilates every holomorphic moment exactly. Separation of the degrees makes the corrections compatible with positivity at every stage. Regularized logarithms built from the peak polynomials then have a fixed negative average, which survives all subsequent corrections. Summing these logarithms with a sufficiently large fixed coefficient makes their negative averages dominate the boundary allowance. Low degrees vary little in a centered chart, while high degrees are exponentially small; this gives the uniform derivatives through order four needed for curvature.

The geometric task has its own delicate point. To prove negative curvature, we must show that its negated numerator is positive on every real two-plane. After rescaling the base directions, the leading expression is nonnegative but can vanish on planes with nonzero base and fiber projections. On these limiting planes the contribution of the potentially largest error term vanishes as well. This cancellation gives stronger error control near the degenerate planes and proves strict negativity for one sufficiently large base parameter $\lambda$. With $\lambda$ fixed, compactness on bounded fiber ranges and a uniform limiting tensor at the fiber boundary give both finite curvature bounds.

Section <a href="#sec:reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:reduction">2</a> states the two precise ingredients and derives Theorem <a href="#main:theorem" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:theorem">1</a> from them by Cauchy estimates for a putative Jacobian. Sections <a href="#sec:peaks" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:peaks">3</a>–<a href="#sec:densities" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:densities">4</a> construct the peaks and positive representing densities; Sections <a href="#sec:tests" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:tests">5</a>–<a href="#sec:base-potential" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:base-potential">6</a> establish their logarithmic tests and the controlled potential. Section <a href="#sec:hartogs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:hartogs">7</a> proves completeness. Sections <a href="#sec:curvature" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:curvature">8</a>–<a href="#sec:pinching" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:pinching">9</a> compute the curvature and prove the two-sided estimate, including the limiting planes just described.

<a id="sec:reduction"></a>
### Two inputs and the bounded-map obstruction

The construction separates a problem about functions on the ball from a curvature estimate. The first input is a strictly plurisubharmonic function with controlled derivatives and an obstruction to a prescribed holomorphic real-part bound. The second turns its complex Hessian bounds into a complete metric with uniformly negative real sectional curvature. We state both inputs precisely and then prove their implication for Theorem <a href="#main:theorem" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:theorem">1</a>.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Centered charts and the two propositions

<a id="base:charts"></a>
Let $\mathbb B=\{z\in\mathbb C^2:|z|<1\}$ and put $$\psi(z)=-\log(1-|z|^2),\qquad
 I=(\psi_{i\bar j}).$$ Here a complex Hessian is also viewed as a Hermitian form, linear in its first argument. To center coordinates at a point $z_0\in\mathbb B$, let $r=|z_0|$, choose a unitary map $U$ with $U(r,0)=z_0$, and set $$\label{base:charts}
 T=U\circ T_r,\qquad
 T_r(\zeta)=\left(
 \frac{r+\zeta_1}{1+r\zeta_1},
 \frac{\sqrt{1-r^2}\,\zeta_2}{1+r\zeta_1}
 \right).$$ These maps are ball automorphisms with $T(0)=z_0$. The identity $$1-|T_r(\zeta)|^2
 =\frac{(1-r^2)(1-|\zeta|^2)}{|1+r\zeta_1|^2}$$ shows that $T^*I$ has the same expression in $\zeta$ as $I$ has in $z$: the additional terms in $\psi\circ T$ are constant or pluriharmonic. In particular, this matrix is the identity at $0$ and has zero first derivatives there. We call <a href="#base:charts" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="base:charts">[base:charts]</a> the centered ball charts.

<a id="base:control"></a>
<a id="base:jets"></a>
For a smooth real function $\phi$ on $\mathbb B$, write $h=\partial\bar\partial\phi$ and, in a centered chart $T$, write $$h^T_{i\bar j}(\zeta)
 =\partial_{\zeta_i}\partial_{\bar\zeta_j}(\phi\circ T)(\zeta).$$ The uniform control needed below is the existence of constants $0<c\le C<\infty$ such that, for every centered chart, $$\label{base:control}
 c\,\mathrm{Id}\le h^T(0)\le C\,\mathrm{Id}$$ and $$\label{base:jets}
 \left|\partial_k h^T_{i\bar j}(0)\right|
 +\left|\partial_{\bar\ell}h^T_{i\bar j}(0)\right|
 +\left|\partial_k\partial_{\bar\ell}h^T_{i\bar j}(0)\right|
 \le C
 \qquad(i,j,k,\ell\in\{1,2\}).$$ The derivatives here are ordinary derivatives of the displayed component functions. In invariant form, <a href="#base:control" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="base:control">[base:control]</a> says $cI\le h\le CI$.

<a id="stmt-base-existence"></a>
<div id="base:existence" class="proposition">

<a id="base:no-minorant"></a>
**Proposition 2** (A controlled potential). *There exist $\phi\in C^\infty(\mathbb B,\mathbb R)$ and a finite constant $C$ for which <a href="#base:control" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="base:control">[base:control]</a> and <a href="#base:jets" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="base:jets">[base:jets]</a> hold with $c=1$, and such that no $H\in\mathcal O(\mathbb B)$ and $C_H\in\mathbb R$ satisfy $$\label{base:no-minorant}
 \operatorname{Re}H(z)
 \le C_H+4\log\frac1{1-|z|}+\phi(z)
 \qquad(z\in\mathbb B).$$*

</div>

<a id="metric:domain"></a>
<a id="metric:potential"></a>
For any smooth real $\phi$ on $\mathbb B$, define its Hartogs domain by $$\label{metric:domain}
 M_\phi=\{(z,w)\in\mathbb B\times\mathbb C:t(z,w)<1\},
 \qquad t(z,w)=e^{\phi(z)}|w|^2.$$ For $\lambda>0$ define the potential and its complex Hessian by $$\label{metric:potential}
 \mathcal K_\lambda=\lambda\psi-\log(1-t),\qquad
 \widehat g_\lambda=\partial\bar\partial\mathcal K_\lambda.$$ Our real metric convention is $g_\lambda=2\operatorname{Re}\widehat g_\lambda$: if a real vector $X$ has $(1,0)$ part $u$, then $g_\lambda(X,X)=2\widehat g_\lambda(u,u)$.

<a id="stmt-metric-transfer"></a>
<div id="metric:transfer" class="proposition">

**Proposition 3** (Hartogs metric transfer). *Let $\phi\in C^\infty(\mathbb B,\mathbb R)$ satisfy <a href="#base:control" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="base:control">[base:control]</a> and <a href="#base:jets" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="base:jets">[base:jets]</a> for fixed $0<c\le C<\infty$. There is a finite $\lambda_0=\lambda_0(c,C)>0$ such that, for every $\lambda\ge\lambda_0$, the domain $M_\phi$ in <a href="#metric:domain" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="metric:domain">[metric:domain]</a> is contractible and the tensor $g_\lambda=2\operatorname{Re}\widehat g_\lambda$ defined by <a href="#metric:potential" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="metric:potential">[metric:potential]</a> is a smooth geodesically complete Kähler metric. For this fixed $\lambda$ there are constants $0<A_\lambda\le B_\lambda<\infty$ such that $$-B_\lambda\le K_{g_\lambda}(\sigma)\le-A_\lambda<0$$ for every point of $M_\phi$ and every real two-plane $\sigma$ in its tangent space.*

</div>

The proof of Proposition <a href="#base:existence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="base:existence">2</a> occupies the analytic construction below. The global geometry and curvature calculation in Sections <a href="#sec:hartogs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:hartogs">7</a>–<a href="#sec:pinching" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:pinching">9</a> prove Proposition <a href="#metric:transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="metric:transfer">3</a>. We first establish the short argument that connects their conclusions to bounded holomorphic maps.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### The Jacobian on the zero section

<a id="stmt-map-obstruction"></a>
<div id="map:obstruction" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4**. *Let $\phi\in C^\infty(\mathbb B,\mathbb R)$ have the nonexistence property in Proposition <a href="#base:existence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="base:existence">2</a>: no holomorphic $H$ and real constant $C_H$ satisfy <a href="#base:no-minorant" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="base:no-minorant">[base:no-minorant]</a>. Then no bounded holomorphic map $F:M_\phi\to\mathbb C^3$ has a nowhere-vanishing complex Jacobian determinant.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-map-obstruction"><summary>Proof of stmt-map-obstruction</summary>

*Proof.* Suppose that $F=(F_1,F_2,F_3)$ is such a map, and choose $B\ge1$ so that $|F_\ell|\le B$ on $M_\phi$ for every component.
